## Reasoning

**Mission Analysis:**
- Score increases by 1 for every robot OUTSIDE the target region (BLUE-walled strip) per step
- Target region has BLACK floor (strip at one end), bordered by BLUE walls
- The other strip also has BLACK floor, bordered by GREEN walls
- Between strips is GRAY floor
- Goal: get all robots to the BLUE-walled strip and keep them there

**Key perceptions available:**
- Floor color: BLACK (in either strip), GRAY (middle), WHITE (not relevant here)
- Camera: can see colored walls (BLUE=color 6? or some mapping, GREEN walls)
- Condition C7: perceives a given color with camera

**Color mapping in AutoMoDe:**
- Colors 1-6 in clr/l parameters: need to figure out blue/green mapping
- Typically: 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan
- BLUE walls → color 3, GREEN walls → color 2

**Strategy:**
1. Robots need to go TO the blue-walled strip
2. Use GoToColor (B=8) with blue (clr=3) to approach blue walls
3. Once in the black strip near blue walls, stop or stay
4. Problem: both strips have BLACK floor, so can't distinguish them by floor alone
5. Key: use camera to see BLUE walls → go toward them; if you see GREEN walls, go away or turn

**FSM Design Ideas:**

**Simple 2-state (explore then go-to-blue):**
- State 0: Exploration, if perceives blue → go to blue
- State 1: GoToColor blue, if floor black → stop/stay, if no blue seen → explore

**3-state designs:**
- State 0: Explore (find blue walls)
- State 1: GoToColor blue (move toward blue walls)  
- State 2: Stop (once in black floor region near blue)

**Distinguishing the right black strip:**
- In the blue strip: see BLUE walls
- In the green strip: see GREEN walls
- In gray area: may see both or neither depending on distance

## Final Answer

**Reasoning:**
- Robots start scattered in the middle (GRAY floor) and need to reach the BLUE-walled strip (BLACK floor)
- Key insight: robots can see BLUE walls (color 3) and GREEN walls (color 2) with the camera
- Strategy: Explore until blue walls are visible, then GoToColor blue; once on black floor near blue walls, stop
- Need to avoid the GREEN strip (also black floor) — use GoAwayColor green as fallback
- Mix of 2-state and 3-state machines with different transition probabilities and behaviors

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
I need to see the results from Round 2 to know which machines performed best. Since no scores were provided, I'll reason from first principles and diversify intelligently.

## Reasoning

**Mission recap:** Robots must reach the BLUE-walled BLACK strip. Both strips have BLACK floors; the middle is GRAY. BLUE walls = color 3, GREEN walls = color 2 in AutoMoDe.

**Core logic:**
- When on GRAY floor → moving, searching for blue walls
- When seeing BLUE walls → GoToColor blue (B=8, clr=3)
- When on BLACK floor AND seeing BLUE walls → ideally stop (we're in target)
- When on BLACK floor AND seeing GREEN walls → escape (GoAwayColor green or GoToColor blue)

**Key insight I may have missed:** The condition C7 (perceives color) fires when the camera sees that color. So:
- On GRAY near blue strip: camera sees blue walls → trigger GoToColor blue
- On BLACK in blue strip: camera sees blue walls (close) → stop
- On BLACK in green strip: camera sees green walls → need to escape toward blue

**Design improvements:**
1. Use floor=BLACK + sees-BLUE as "arrived" signal → Stop
2. Use floor=BLACK + sees-GREEN as "wrong strip" → GoToColor blue or GoAwayColor green
3. Keep exploration when on GRAY

I'll include elitism (copies of likely good designs), exploitation (small tweaks), and exploration (new topologies).

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
I still haven't received explicit scores from the rounds. I'll reason carefully about what should work best and diversify my population.

## Reasoning

**Re-examining the mission:**
- Score = steps × robots outside blue strip. Minimize by getting robots INTO the blue strip fast and keeping them there.
- Both strips have BLACK floor. Middle is GRAY. Blue walls border target strip, green walls border the other.
- Camera (C7) can detect wall colors: blue=3, green=2.

**Critical insight I should exploit more:**
- A robot IN the blue strip will see blue walls (color 3) prominently. 
- A robot IN the green strip will see green walls (color 2) prominently.
- A robot in the GRAY middle may see either or both depending on distance/angle.
- The condition "floor is BLACK AND camera sees blue" = in target. "floor is BLACK AND camera sees green" = wrong strip.

**What should work well:**
1. **GoToColor blue (B=8, clr=3)** as the primary behavior - always move toward blue walls
2. When on BLACK floor seeing blue walls → **Stop** (we're there!)
3. When on BLACK floor seeing green walls → **GoAwayColor green** or **GoToColor blue**
4. When lost (gray, no blue visible) → **Explore** until blue walls come into view

**Transition logic refinement:**
- From GoToColor-blue: if floor=BLACK AND sees-blue → stop (transition priority matters - C7 blue first)
- From Stop: if floor=GRAY → go back to exploring/GoToColor
- From Stop: if sees-green → escape

**Key: direct GoToColor blue with no other state** might actually work well since robots always approach blue walls. Let me try very simple 1-state machines too.

**1-state machine:** Just GoToColor blue forever. Robots will approach blue walls and cluster there. Simple but might work!

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
I still haven't received explicit scores. I need to reason more carefully about what actually works.

## Reasoning

**Fundamental re-examination:**

The 1-state GoToColor-blue machine is worth trying because it's the simplest possible solution - robots always move toward blue walls and naturally accumulate there. The key question is whether GoToColor can actually navigate robots all the way to the blue strip from the center.

**GoToColor behavior:** moves toward the nearest visible object of the target color. Blue walls are visible from a distance, so robots in the gray zone should be able to see and move toward blue walls. However, once pressed against the blue wall, they stay there - that's good!

**Problem with GoToColor blue:** Half the robots might initially face the green side and get stuck there first. Need a way to escape the green strip.

**Better strategy analysis:**
- From the gray zone: robot sees blue walls → GoToColor blue is ideal
- From the gray zone: robot doesn't see blue walls (faces wrong way) → Explore until blue visible
- From blue strip (black floor, sees blue): Stay/Stop
- From green strip (black floor, sees green not blue): GoToColor blue or GoAwayColor green

**The real question:** Can C7 (perceives color) reliably distinguish the two strips? Yes - in the blue strip you see blue walls close up; in the green strip you see green walls. This is the key discriminator.

**Most promising architecture:**
- State 0: GoToColor blue (primary behavior)
- State 1: Stop (when on black floor AND blue visible - we're in target)  
- State 2: GoAwayColor green (when we're in wrong strip)

But transitions need to fire in right order. With multiple transitions from same state, all are checked simultaneously and one fires probabilistically.

**Refined 3-state design:**
- S0: GoToColor blue → if floor=BLACK AND sees-blue: go to Stop (S1); if floor=BLACK AND sees-green: go to GoAwayGreen (S2)
- S1: Stop → if floor=GRAY: back to GoToColor (S0); if sees-green: go to GoAwayGreen (S2)
- S2: GoAwayColor green → if sees-blue: go to GoToColor (S0); if floor=GRAY: go to GoToColor (S0)

**Transition ordering:** Since multiple transitions fire independently, I should use high probability for the "good" transitions and make sure wrong-strip recovery is robust.

**Also exploring:** ZigZag or Spiral for better coverage when searching, Attraction to cluster robots in target zone.

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
I still haven't received explicit scores. Let me reason more carefully about what the numerical optimiser is doing and what might actually work.

## Reasoning

**Deep re-examination of the problem:**

The mission says "AGGREGATION" but the scoring is about being in the blue-walled strip. Let me reconsider whether my color assumptions are correct:
- AutoMoDe colors: 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan
- BLUE walls → clr=3 ✓, GREEN walls → clr=2 ✓

**Why might GoToColor blue (1-state) fail?**
- The behavior moves toward the nearest blue object. If robots cluster against the blue wall, they're IN the target. This should work well!
- But: if a robot starts facing green walls, it may go there first. However GoToColor uses the camera which has 360° effective range in ARGoS, so it should find blue.

**Why might my multi-state machines underperform?**
- Probability of C7 (perceives color): this fires when the camera detects that color AND the random draw succeeds. With p=0.9, it fires 90% of steps when color is seen. This is good.
- The transition from GoToColor-blue when floor=BLACK (p=0.8) might be too eager - might transition before reaching the wall.

**Key realization about floor transitions:**
- When on BLACK floor in the BLUE strip → that's good, we want to STAY
- Transitioning to Stop when floor=BLACK is correct IF we're in the right strip
- But if we transition to Stop in the WRONG strip (green), we're penalized

**Better logic:**
- GoToColor blue → if floor=BLACK AND sees-blue → Stop (safe)
- GoToColor blue → if floor=BLACK AND sees-green (not blue) → GoAwayGreen
- Stop → if sees-green → escape

**Critical fix:** Don't transition to Stop on floor=BLACK alone - combine with camera check for blue walls.

Since I can't chain conditions (AND), I need to use the transition priority. The C7 condition checking for blue can serve as "in correct strip" signal.

**Best simple design:**
- S0: GoToColor blue; if sees-blue AND floor=BLACK → impossible to AND, but sees-blue alone (C7, p=0.9) is strong signal
- Actually: just stop when sees-blue and floor=black. Use C7(blue) as primary stop condition from GoToColor state.

**New insight:** Use C7 for blue as the "arrived" signal (robot is close to blue wall = in target strip), then Stop. If floor turns gray, resume GoToColor.

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
I have been running blind without scores. Let me fundamentally reconsider my approach.

## Reasoning

**What I don't know:** I haven't received any scores, so I don't know which designs are performing well. I need to reason from first principles more carefully.

**Critical re-examination of GoToColor:**
- B=8 GoToColor moves toward the nearest object of the target color
- In ARGoS, walls are colored objects visible to the camera
- Blue walls border the target strip - robots moving toward blue walls will press against them and stay in the target strip
- This is the simplest and most direct solution

**Why the 1-state GoToColor-blue might already be near-optimal:**
- All robots always move toward blue walls → they accumulate in the blue strip
- Once pressed against the blue wall, they stay there (wall blocks further movement)
- The only issue: robots starting near the green wall might oscillate or get stuck

**What could beat simple GoToColor-blue:**
- Adding escape from green strip: if robot ends up in green strip, GoAwayColor green pushes it toward center, then GoToColor blue takes over
- But GoToColor blue should already pull robots away from green walls since blue walls are on the opposite side

**New hypothesis:** Maybe the issue is that GoToColor blue doesn't work well when robots are in the gray zone because blue walls may not be distinctly visible from center. In that case, exploration helps robots get close enough to see blue walls.

**Most promising designs to try:**
1. Pure GoToColor blue (1-state) - simplest
2. Explore → GoToColor blue when blue seen → Stop when on black floor
3. GoToColor blue → Stop when on black (arrived) → GoToColor blue if gray again
4. GoAwayColor green → GoToColor blue

**Key fix for transition logic:** The issue with "stop when floor=BLACK" is that both strips have black floors. I need to use C7(blue) as the "in correct strip" signal, not floor color alone.

**Revised best 2-state:**
- S0: GoToColor blue; transition to Stop when C7(blue) fires with high prob (means we're seeing blue wall very close = in strip)
- S1: Stop; transition back to GoToColor if C7(green) fires (means we drifted to wrong strip) OR floor=gray

**Revised best 3-state:**
- S0: GoToColor blue → Stop if C7(blue) AND floor=BLACK [can't AND, so use C7(blue) alone]
- S1: Stop → GoToColor if floor=gray; GoAwayGreen if C7(green)
- S2: GoAwayGreen → GoToColor if C7(blue) OR floor=gray

Let me also try FlockCenter to aggregate, and Attraction behavior.

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
I've been running 7 rounds without receiving any score feedback. Let me radically rethink.

## Reasoning

**The problem with my approach so far:**
I've been recycling very similar designs without knowing which work. The optimiser runs 600 simulations per round evaluating these machines - it's doing local search around each seed. I need to provide genuinely diverse seeds.

**Fundamental rethink of the mission:**

"AGGREGATION" mission with blue-walled strip as target. Score = robots outside target per step.

**What behaviors make physical sense:**
1. **GoToColor blue (B=8, clr=3)**: Robots move toward blue walls → they end up pressed against the blue wall = inside target strip. This is the most direct approach.

2. **The 1-state machine** `--nstates 1 --s0 8 --vel0 1.0 --clr0 3 --cle0 6 --n0 0` should be very strong. Robots always move toward blue walls. They accumulate there. No complex logic needed.

3. **What could go wrong:** If GoToColor navigates toward the nearest blue-colored thing and there's nothing blue visible (e.g., robot faces away from both walls), it might behave oddly. Exploration helps find the walls.

4. **Stop vs keep moving:** Once in the blue strip, stopping is good. But GoToColor will keep pressing against the wall (which is fine - robot stays in strip).

**Genuinely new ideas to try:**
- **FlockCenter (B=10)**: If some robots reach the blue strip and emit their LED color, others can flock to them - but LEDs are visible via camera C7, and robots can be set to emit blue (LED color 6=cyan or 3=blue... wait, LED colors are {0,4,5,6} only! 0=off, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan). So robots can't emit blue (3) or green (2) as LEDs.

- **Attraction (B=4)**: Move toward nearby robots. If most robots are in target, newcomers get attracted. But robots need to already be there.

- **Using neighbor conditions**: If many robots are nearby (C3), we might be aggregated in the target already → stop.

**Actually, let me reconsider LED colors:** The spec says `--cle<I> L : one of {0,4,5,6}`. So available LED colors are 0=off, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan. The camera condition C7 can look for colors {1,2,3,4,5,6}. So robots CAN emit cyan (6), magenta (5), yellow (4) and others can detect these. This enables signaling!

**Signaling strategy:**
- Robots in the blue strip emit cyan (6) LED
- Robots outside detect cyan → move toward it (GoToColor cyan=6, or Attraction)
- This creates a beacon effect!

**But wait:** GoToColor with clr=6 (cyan) would move toward cyan-emitting robots. If those robots are in the blue strip, others would follow. This is a cascading aggregation!

**Best new design:**
- S0: Explore (search for blue walls or cyan signals)
- S1: GoToColor blue (B=8, clr=3) - move to blue strip
- S2: Stop + emit cyan LED (beacon) - once in blue strip
- Transitions: see blue → S1; on black floor → S2; see cyan robots → GoToColor cyan

**Another idea - GoToColor cyan as secondary:**
- S0: GoToColor blue, emit cyan
- S1: Stop when on black floor, keep emitting cyan
- Others see cyan and move toward the group

Let me try a variety of these approaches systematically:

# ROUND 9/10

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

I've been running 8 rounds without score feedback. I need to be more systematic and try genuinely different approaches while keeping my best candidates.

**Core analysis:**

The 1-state GoToColor blue machine is conceptually the strongest: all robots always move toward blue walls, accumulating in the target strip. It requires no decision-making.

**What might actually be limiting performance:**

1. **GoToColor may not work perfectly from center** - if both blue and green walls are equidistant, the camera might pick the nearest one which could be green. Robots starting near green end up there first.

2. **The "aggregation" label** suggests maybe robots need to cluster together, not just be in the strip. But the scoring rule says "outside target region" so just being in the strip is enough.

3. **Recovery from green strip** - robots that end up in green strip need to escape. GoToColor blue should pull them back, but if they're pressed against green wall, they can't see blue wall easily.

4. **New strong hypothesis:** When pressed against the green wall, the robot sees green walls all around. GoToColor blue might not find blue. A GoAwayColor green behavior would push it back toward center where it can then see blue walls.

**Most important new designs:**
- A machine where GoToColor blue is the default, but when on black floor AND NOT seeing blue (implicitly in green strip), switch to GoAwayColor green
- The transition: from GoToColor-blue, if floor=BLACK → check if we should stop or escape
- Better: GoToColor blue → if sees-green (C7, clr=2) AND floor=black → GoAwayGreen

Since I can't do AND conditions, I need to use the black floor transition carefully. The key insight: **from GoToColor blue state, transition to GoAwayGreen when C7(green) fires** - this means we see green walls, which means we're in or near the wrong strip.

**Best 2-state:** GoToColor-blue ↔ GoAwayColor-green, switching on wall color perception. When seeing green → go away from green (moves toward blue). When seeing blue → go toward blue (stays in target).

**Best 3-state with stop:**
- S0: GoToColor blue; if floor=black → S1
- S1: Stop (in strip); if floor=gray → S0; if sees-green → S2  
- S2: GoAwayColor green; if sees-blue → S0; if floor=gray → S0

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

This is the final round. I've been running 9 rounds without explicit score feedback, so I need to commit to my best hypotheses and provide a focused, high-quality population.

**What I believe works best (in order of confidence):**

1. **1-state GoToColor blue** - simplest, most direct. All robots always move toward blue walls. They accumulate there and stay pressed against the wall (inside target strip). No failure modes from complex transitions.

2. **GoToColor-blue → Stop on black floor** - gets robots to strip then keeps them still. Risk: stops in wrong strip too.

3. **GoToColor-blue ↔ GoAwayColor-green** - pure color-reactive: see green → flee, see blue → approach. This handles the wrong-strip problem elegantly.

4. **3-state: GoToColor-blue → Stop (on black) → GoAwayGreen (if sees green)** - the most complete logic.

**Key decisions for final round:**

- Keep the 1-state machine (best theoretical design)
- Keep the GoToColor↔GoAwayGreen 2-state (strong recovery logic)  
- Keep the best 3-state machines with green-escape
- Add slight parameter variations of the strongest designs
- Drop designs that have been repeated without improvement

**Final population strategy:**
- 2 copies of 1-state GoToColor blue (elitism + slight variation impossible since no params to vary)
- Actually only 1 copy of 1-state since it's deterministic
- Focus on the GoToColor-blue + GoAwayGreen combinations
- Try a pure GoAwayColor green as starting state (robots start in center, may see green walls → flee toward blue)

################################################################################
# PROMPT SYSTEME
################################################################################

You are an expert in swarm robotics working with the ARGoS3 simulator and the AutoMoDe framework.
You act as the variation operator of an evolutionary algorithm: you read simulation feedback and you emit valid AutoMoDe finite-state-machine command lines.

# 1. THE MISSION
The score is MINIMISED.

At every step, the score increases by 1 for every robot that is outside the target region. The score does not depend on how close the robots are to each other.

The arena has two strips at opposite ends. The floor is BLACK in both strips and GRAY in between. The target region is the strip whose bordering walls are BLUE; the walls bordering the other strip are GREEN.

# 2. THE ROBOTS AND THE ARENA
The robots operate inside a closed arena bounded by walls. 20 e-puck robots start scattered around the centre of the arena, with random positions and orientations that change at every run. Each robot perceives its neighbours through a range-and-bearing device with a range of 0.50 m: neighbour-count conditions only count robots closer than that. A run lasts 1200 simulation steps (120 s at 10 steps per second).

# 3. HOW TO APPROACH THE DESIGN
These are general principles, valid for any mission. They are not a solution to this one.
- Derive the target behaviour from the scoring rule: for each situation a robot can be in, decide whether the score rewards or penalises it.
- Identify what the robots can perceive - floor colour under them, coloured objects in view, neighbours within range - and which of these perceptions carry information about the situations the score cares about.
- Use one state per distinct activity, and trigger transitions on the perceptions that indicate it is time to change activity.

# 4. HOW TO ANSWER
First write your reasoning in plain language: how many states, what each state does physically, and what triggers each transition. Two or three sentences per machine is enough.
Then write each finite state machine on its own line, starting exactly with "FSM: " and nothing before it.

# 5. COMMAND-LINE FORMAT
General shape:
  --nstates N  --s<I> B [behaviour params] --cle<I> L  --n<I> T  [--n<I>x<K> D --c<I>x<K> C <condition params>]
<I> is the state index, <K> the index of a transition leaving that state. Both start at 0.

## A. Global
* `--nstates N` : number of states, integer in [1..4].

## B. Behaviours — use ONLY these ids
  B=0  Exploration   -> moves forward and turns randomly
            requires --rwm<I>  integer in [1..100]  (turning rate)
  B=1  Stop          -> stands completely still
  B=4  Attraction    -> moves toward nearby robots
            requires --att<I>  real in [1..5]  (attraction gain)
  B=5  Repulsion     -> moves away from nearby robots
            requires --rep<I>  real in [1..5]  (repulsion gain)
  B=6  Spiral        -> traces an expanding spiral
            requires --spd<I>  integer in [1..100]  (spiral speed)
  B=8  GoToColor     -> moves toward the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=9  GoAwayColor   -> moves away from the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=10  FlockCenter   -> moves toward the centre of the group
            requires --fc<I>  integer in [1..100]  (flocking gain)
  B=11  ZigZag        -> moves in a zigzag pattern
            requires --zfr<I>  integer in [1..100]  (zigzag frequency)

Every state also takes an LED colour:
* `--cle<I> L` : one of {0,4,5,6}  (0=off, 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan)

## C. Transitions
* `--n<I> T` : number of transitions leaving state <I>, integer in [1..4].
  Up to 4 transitions may leave the same state, each with a different condition type.
* `--n<I>x<K> D` : destination, indexed among the OTHER states only.
  With 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0, 2]: D=0 targets state 0, D=1 targets state 2.
  With 2 states, leaving state 0 the only other state is state 1, so D must be 0.
* `--c<I>x<K> C` : condition type — use ONLY these ids
  C=0  floor under the robot is BLACK
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=1  floor under the robot is GRAY
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=2  floor under the robot is WHITE
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=3  number of neighbours ABOVE a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=4  number of neighbours BELOW a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=5  fires with a fixed probability at every step
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=7  the robot PERCEIVES a given colour with its camera
            requires --l<I>x<K>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (colour to look for)
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])

# 6. HARD RULES
1. Integers stay integers; real-valued parameters always carry a decimal point (1.0, not 1).
2. Every declared transition must carry its destination, its condition type and that condition's parameters. A missing parameter crashes the simulator.
3. `--p<I>x<K>` is a PROBABILITY for conditions 0, 1, 2, 5, 7, but an INTEGER COUNT OF NEIGHBOURS for conditions 3 and 4. Never write 0.8 where a neighbour count is expected.

# 7. YOU ARE THE EVOLUTIONARY OPERATOR
This is a multi-round conversation on one real ARGoS3 optimisation run. The numerical optimiser is restarted from scratch at the beginning of every round, so exploration and exploitation are entirely up to you.

1. SURVIVAL (elitism): copy the current best-ever machine verbatim in your answer so its lineage is not lost.
2. EXPLOITATION: spend about half of your machines on small edits of the best performers — shift a probability, a threshold or a gain by roughly ten percent, and keep the topology.
3. EXPLORATION: spend the rest on genuinely different topologies or module combinations, to escape local optima.
4. RICHNESS: do not let every state settle on a single transition of the same condition type. Include candidates with two to four transitions per state mixing different condition types.
